<ROLE>
Your primary role is to assist users by executing commands, modifying code, and solving technical problems effectively. You should be thorough, methodical, and prioritize quality over speed.
* If the user asks a question, like "why is X happening", don't try to fix the problem. Just give an answer to the question.
</ROLE>

<EFFICIENCY>
* Each action you take is somewhat expensive. Wherever possible, combine multiple actions into a single action, e.g. combine multiple bash commands into one, using sed and grep to edit/view multiple files at once.
* When exploring the codebase, use efficient tools like find, grep, and git commands with appropriate filters to minimize unnecessary operations.
</EFFICIENCY>

<FILE_SYSTEM_GUIDELINES>
* When a user provides a file path, do NOT assume it's relative to the current working directory. First explore the file system to locate the file before working on it.
* If asked to edit a file, edit the file directly, rather than creating a new file with a different filename.
* For global search-and-replace operations, consider using `sed` instead of opening file editors multiple times.
* NEVER create multiple versions of the same file with different suffixes (e.g., file_test.py, file_fix.py, file_simple.py). Instead:
  - Always modify the original file directly when making changes
  - If you need to create a temporary file for testing, delete it once you've confirmed your solution works
  - If you decide a file you created is no longer useful, delete it instead of creating a new version
* Do NOT include documentation files explaining your changes in version control unless the user explicitly requests it
* When reproducing bugs or implementing fixes, use a single file rather than creating multiple files with different versions
</FILE_SYSTEM_GUIDELINES>

<CODE_QUALITY>
* Write clean, efficient code with minimal comments. Avoid redundancy in comments: Do not repeat information that can be easily inferred from the code itself.
* When implementing solutions, focus on making the minimal changes needed to solve the problem.
* Before implementing any changes, first thoroughly understand the codebase through exploration.
* If you are adding a lot of code to a function or file, consider splitting the function or file into smaller pieces when appropriate.
* Place all imports at the top of the file unless explicitly requested otherwise or if placing imports at the top would cause issues (e.g., circular imports, conditional imports, or imports that need to be delayed for specific reasons).
</CODE_QUALITY>

<VERSION_CONTROL>
* If there are existing git user credentials already configured, use them and add Co-authored-by: openhands <openhands@all-hands.dev> to any commits messages you make. if a git config doesn't exist use "openhands" as the user.name and "openhands@all-hands.dev" as the user.email by default, unless explicitly instructed otherwise.
* Exercise caution with git operations. Do NOT make potentially dangerous changes (e.g., pushing to main, deleting repositories) unless explicitly asked to do so.
* When committing changes, use `git status` to see all modified files, and stage all files necessary for the commit. Use `git commit -a` whenever possible.
* Do NOT commit files that typically shouldn't go into version control (e.g., node_modules/, .env files, build directories, cache files, large binaries) unless explicitly instructed by the user.
* If unsure about committing certain files, check for the presence of .gitignore files or ask the user for clarification.
</VERSION_CONTROL>

<PULL_REQUESTS>
* **Important**: Do not push to the remote branch and/or start a pull request unless explicitly asked to do so.
* When creating pull requests, create only ONE per session/issue unless explicitly instructed otherwise.
* When working with an existing PR, update it with new commits rather than creating additional PRs for the same issue.
* When updating a PR, preserve the original PR title and purpose, updating description only when necessary.
</PULL_REQUESTS>

<PROBLEM_SOLVING_WORKFLOW>
1. EXPLORATION: Thoroughly explore relevant files and understand the context before proposing solutions
2. ANALYSIS: Consider multiple approaches and select the most promising one
3. TESTING:
   * For bug fixes: Create tests to verify issues before implementing fixes
   * For new features: Consider test-driven development when appropriate
   * Do NOT write tests for documentation changes, README updates, configuration files, or other non-functionality changes
   * If the repository lacks testing infrastructure and implementing tests would require extensive setup, consult with the user before investing time in building testing infrastructure
   * If the environment is not set up to run tests, consult with the user first before investing time to install all dependencies
4. IMPLEMENTATION:

   * Always modify existing files directly rather than creating new versions with different suffixes

</PROBLEM_SOLVING_WORKFLOW>

<SECURITY>
* Only use GITHUB_TOKEN and other credentials in ways the user has explicitly requested and would expect.

<EXTERNAL_SERVICES>

<ENVIRONMENT_SETUP>
* When user asks you to run an application, don't stop if the application is not installed. Instead, please install the application and run the command again.
* If you encounter missing dependencies:
  1. First, look around in the repository for existing dependency files (requirements.txt, pyproject.toml, package.json, Gemfile, etc.)
  2. If dependency files exist, use them to install all dependencies at once (e.g., `pip install -r requirements.txt`, `npm install`, etc.)
  3. Only install individual packages directly if no dependency files are found or if only specific packages are needed
* Similarly, if you encounter missing dependencies for essential tools requested by the user, install them when possible.
</ENVIRONMENT_SETUP>

<TROUBLESHOOTING>
* If you've made repeated attempts to solve a problem but tests still fail or the user reports it's still broken:
  1. Step back and reflect on 5-7 different possible sources of the problem
  2. Assess the likelihood of each possible cause
  3. Methodically address the most likely causes, starting with the highest probability
  4. Document your reasoning process
* When you run into any major issue while executing a plan from the user, please don't try to directly work around it. Instead, propose a new plan and confirm with the user before proceeding.
</TROUBLESHOOTING>

<DOCUMENTATION>
* When explaining changes or solutions to the user:
  - Include explanations in your conversation responses rather than creating separate documentation files
  - If you need to create documentation files for reference, do NOT include them in version control unless explicitly requested
  - Never create multiple versions of documentation files with different suffixes
* If the user asks for documentation:
  - Confirm whether they want it as a separate file or just in the conversation
  - Ask if they want documentation files to be included in version control
</DOCUMENTATION>

<PROCESS_MANAGEMENT>
* When terminating processes:
  - Do NOT use general keywords with commands like `pkill -f server` or `pkill -f python` as this might accidentally kill other important servers or processes
  - Always use specific keywords that uniquely identify the target process
  - Prefer using `ps aux` to find the exact process ID (PID) first, then kill that specific PID
  - When possible, use more targeted approaches like finding the PID from a pidfile or using application-specific shutdown commands
</PROCESS_MANAGEMENT>

  1. Begin by decomposing the overall objective into primary phases using `task_tracker`

  3. Update tasks to "in_progress" status when commencing work on them
  4. Update tasks to "done" status immediately after completing each item

    20	def build_repr(instance, fields):
    21	    """
    22	    Build the string representation for an instance.
    23	
    24	    Args:
    25	        instance:
    26	            The instance to build the repr for.
    27	        fields:
    28	            A list of fields to include in the repr.
    29	
    30	    Returns:
    31	        A string describing the provided instance including
    32	        representations of all specified fields.
    33	    """
    34	    values = [f"{f}={repr(getattr(instance, f))}" for f in fields]
    35	
    36	    return f'{instance.__class__.__name__}({", ".join(values)})'

    39	def generate_token():
    40	    """
    41	    Create a random token.
    42	
    43	    Returns:
    44	        A random alphanumeric string.
    45	    """
    46	    return crypto.get_random_string(
    47	        allowed_chars=string.ascii_letters + string.digits, length=TOKEN_LENGTH
    48	    )

   132	    def send_already_verified(self):
   133	        """
   134	        Send an email notifying the user that this email address has
   135	        already been verified.
   136	        """
   137	        context = {"email": self}
   138	        template = "email_auth/emails/already-verified"
   139	
   140	        email_utils.send_email(
   141	            context=context,
   142	            from_email=settings.DEFAULT_FROM_EMAIL,
   143	            recipient_list=[self.address],
   144	            subject=_("Your Email Address has Already Been Verified"),
   145	            template_name=template,
   146	        )
   147	
   148	    def send_duplicate_notification(self):
   149	        """
   150	        Send an email notifying the user that this email address has
   151	        already been registered.
   152	        """
   153	        context = {"email": self}
   154	        template = "email_auth/emails/duplicate-email"
   155	
   156	        email_utils.send_email(
   157	            context=context,
   158	            from_email=settings.DEFAULT_FROM_EMAIL,
   159	            recipient_list=[self.address],
   160	            subject=_("Your Email Address has Already Been Registered"),
   161	            template_name=template,
   162	        )
   163	
   164	    def send_verification_email(self):
   165	        """
   166	        Create and send a verification email to the address associated
   167	        with the instance.
   168	
   169	        Returns:
   170	            The created :py:class:`EmailVerification` instance.
   171	        """
   172	        verification = EmailVerification.objects.create(email=self)
   173	        verification.send_email()
   174	
   175	        return verification
   176	
   177	    def verify(self):
   178	        """
   179	        Mark the email address instance as verified.
   180	        """
   181	        self.is_verified = True
   182	        self.time_verified = timezone.now()
   183	
   184	        logger.info("Verified email address %s", self.address)
   185	
   186	        self.save()

   189	class EmailVerification(models.Model):
   190	    """
   191	    A token that allows a user to verify that they own an email address.
   192	
   193	    Ownership is established by emailing the user a random token to the
   194	    email address they provided. If they can provide that token, we
   195	    assume they own the email address it was sent to.
   196	    """
   197	
   198	    email = models.ForeignKey(
   199	        "email_auth.EmailAddress",
   200	        help_text=_("The email address that the token is meant to verify."),
   201	        on_delete=models.CASCADE,
   202	        related_name="verifications",
   203	        related_query_name="verification",
   204	        verbose_name=_("email address"),
   205	    )
   206	    time_created = models.DateTimeField(
   207	        auto_now_add=True,
   208	        help_text=_("The time that the instance was created."),
   209	        verbose_name=_("creation time"),
   210	    )
   211	    time_sent = models.DateTimeField(
   212	        blank=True,
   213	        help_text=_("The time that the token was emailed out."),
   214	        null=True,
   215	        verbose_name=_("sent time"),
   216	    )
   217	    time_updated = models.DateTimeField(
   218	        auto_now=True,
   219	        help_text=_("The time of the last update to the instance."),
   220	        verbose_name=_("last update time"),
   221	    )
   222	    token = models.CharField(
   223	        default=generate_token,
   224	        help_text=_("The random token identifying the verification request."),
   225	        max_length=TOKEN_LENGTH,
   226	        primary_key=True,
   227	        verbose_name=_("token"),
   228	    )
   229	
   230	    class Meta:
   231	        ordering = ("email__normalized_address",)
   232	        verbose_name = _("email verification")
   233	        verbose_name_plural = _("email verifications")
   234	
   235	    def __repr__(self):
   236	        """
   237	        Returns:
   238	            A string representation of the instance suitable for
   239	            debugging purposes.
   240	        """
   241	        return build_repr(
   242	            self,
   243	            ["email", "time_created", "time_sent", "time_updated", "token"],
   244	        )
   245	
   246	    def __str__(self):
   247	        """
   248	        Returns:
   249	            A description of the email address that this verification
   250	            is for.
   251	        """
   252	        return f"Verification for {self.email}"
   253	
   254	    def send_email(self):
   255	        """
   256	        Send an email containing the verification token to the email
   257	        address being verified.
   258	        """
   259	        context = {
   260	            "email": self.email,
   261	            "user": self.email.user,
   262	            "verification": self,
   263	        }
   264	        template = "email_auth/emails/verify-email"
   265	
   266	        email_utils.send_email(
   267	            context=context,
   268	            from_email=settings.DEFAULT_FROM_EMAIL,
   269	            recipient_list=[self.email.address],
   270	            subject=_("Please Verify Your Email Address"),
   271	            template_name=template,
   272	        )
   273	
   274	    def verify(self):
   275	        """
   276	        Mark the associated email address as verified and delete the
   277	        verification instance.
   278	        """
   279	        self.email.verify()
   280	        self.delete()
   281

   283	class PasswordReset(models.Model):

   285	    A model containing a token that can be used to reset a user's

   300	        verbose_name=_("creation time"),
   301	    )

   348	        address associated with the instance.

   355	            recipient_list=[self.email.address],

   357	            template_name="email_auth/emails/reset-password",
   358	        )
   359	
   360	        self.time_sent = timezone.now()

### Problem Analysis (Phase 1):

1.1 **Code Structure**: The EmailVerification model has a `time_sent` field (line 211-216) that should track when the verification email was sent.

1.2 **Technical Details**:
- Model: `EmailVerification` 
- Field: `time_sent` (DateTimeField, nullable)
- Method: `send_email()` (lines 254-272)
- Comparison: `PasswordReset.send_email()` correctly sets `time_sent` (lines 345-361)

1.3 **Problem Statement**: The `EmailVerification.send_email()` method sends the email but never populates the `time_sent` field with the current timestamp and saves the instance.

1.4 **Steps to Reproduce**:
1. Create an EmailVerification instance
2. Call the `send_email()` method
3. Check if `time_sent` field is populated
4. Observe that it remains None/null

1.5 **Best Practices**:
- Follow the pattern used in `PasswordReset.send_email()` which correctly sets `time_sent`
- Use `timezone.now()` for timestamp consistency
- Save the model instance after setting the field
- Make minimal changes to fix the issue

### 3.3 Proposed Solution:

**Files Related to the Problem:**
1. `/workspace/cdriehuys__django-simple-email-auth__0.2/email_auth/models.py` - Contains the EmailVerification model with the problematic `send_email()` method
2. `/workspace/cdriehuys__django-simple-email-auth__0.2/email_auth/test/models/test_email_verification_model.py` - Contains the test for send_email (but doesn't test time_sent)

**Methods to Fix:**
- `EmailVerification.send_email()` method (lines 254-272 in models.py)

**Why This Location:**
Looking at the comparison with `PasswordReset.send_email()` method (lines 345-361), I can see that:
1. `PasswordReset.send_email()` correctly sets `self.time_sent = timezone.now()` and calls `self.save()` (lines 360-361)
2. `EmailVerification.send_email()` is missing these two lines
3. Both methods follow the same pattern of sending emails using `email_utils.send_email()`

**Most Likely Location:** `/workspace/cdriehuys__django-simple-email-auth__0.2/email_auth/models.py` lines 254-272

def test_time_sent_population():
    """Test that time_sent is populated when send_email is called."""
    print("Testing EmailVerification.time_sent population...")
    
    # Create a user and email address
    user = get_user_model().objects.create_user(username='testuser', email='test@example.com')
    email = models.EmailAddress.objects.create(address='test@example.com', user=user)
    
    # Create an EmailVerification instance
    verification = models.EmailVerification.objects.create(email=email)
    
    print(f"Before send_email(): time_sent = {verification.time_sent}")
    
    # Mock the email sending to avoid actual email delivery
    with mock.patch('email_auth.models.email_utils.send_email') as mock_send_email:
        # Call send_email
        verification.send_email()
        
        # Refresh from database to get updated values
        verification.refresh_from_db()
        
        print(f"After send_email(): time_sent = {verification.time_sent}")
        
        # Check if time_sent was populated
        if verification.time_sent is None:
            print("❌ BUG CONFIRMED: time_sent is still None after send_email()")
            return False
        else:
            print("✅ time_sent was correctly populated")
            return True

def test_password_reset_comparison():

"""
Script to reproduce the issue where EmailVerification.time_sent is not populated.

import os
import sys
import django
from django.conf import settings

# Add the project root to the path
sys.path.insert(0, '/workspace/cdriehuys__django-simple-email-auth__0.2')

# Configure Django settings
os.environ.setdefault('DJANGO_SETTINGS_MODULE', 'test_settings')
django.setup()

from unittest import mock
from django.contrib.auth import get_user_model
from django.utils import timezone
from email_auth import models

def test_time_sent_population():
    """Test that time_sent is populated when send_email is called."""
    print("Testing EmailVerification.time_sent population...")
    
    # Create a user and email address
    user = get_user_model().objects.create_user(username='testuser', email='test@example.com')
    email = models.EmailAddress.objects.create(address='test@example.com', user=user)
    
    # Create an EmailVerification instance
    verification = models.EmailVerification.objects.create(email=email)
    
    print(f"Before send_email(): time_sent = {verification.time_sent}")
    
    # Mock the email sending to avoid actual email delivery
    with mock.patch('email_auth.models.email_utils.send_email') as mock_send_email:
        # Call send_email
        verification.send_email()
        
        # Refresh from database to get updated values
        verification.refresh_from_db()
        
        print(f"After send_email(): time_sent = {verification.time_sent}")
        
        # Check if time_sent was populated
        if verification.time_sent is None:
            print("❌ BUG CONFIRMED: time_sent is still None after send_email()")
            return False
        else:
            print("✅ time_sent was correctly populated")
            return True

def test_password_reset_comparison():
    """Test PasswordReset for comparison - it should work correctly."""
    print("\nTesting PasswordReset.time_sent population (should work)...")
    
    # Create a user and email address
    user = get_user_model().objects.create_user(username='testuser2', email='test2@example.com')
    email = models.EmailAddress.objects.create(address='test2@example.com', user=user)
    
    # Create a PasswordReset instance
    password_reset = models.PasswordReset.objects.create(email=email)
    
    print(f"Before send_email(): time_sent = {password_reset.time_sent}")
    
    # Mock the email sending to avoid actual email delivery
    with mock.patch('email_auth.models.email_utils.send_email') as mock_send_email:
        # Call send_email
        password_reset.send_email()
        
        # Refresh from database to get updated values
        password_reset.refresh_from_db()
        
        print(f"After send_email(): time_sent = {password_reset.time_sent}")
        
        # Check if time_sent was populated
        if password_reset.time_sent is None:
            print("❌ Unexpected: PasswordReset time_sent is None")
            return False
        else:
            print("✅ PasswordReset time_sent was correctly populated")
            return True

if __name__ == '__main__':
    print("=" * 60)
    print("REPRODUCING EMAIL VERIFICATION TIME_SENT ISSUE")
    print("=" * 60)
    
    email_verification_works = test_time_sent_population()
    password_reset_works = test_password_reset_comparison()
    
    print("\n" + "=" * 60)
    print("SUMMARY:")
    print(f"EmailVerification time_sent populated: {'YES' if email_verification_works else 'NO'}")
    print(f"PasswordReset time_sent populated: {'YES' if password_reset_works else 'NO'}")

"""
Simple script to reproduce the time_sent issue using the same pattern as the tests.
"""

from unittest import mock
from django.contrib.auth import get_user_model
from django.utils import timezone
from email_auth import models


def test_email_verification_time_sent():
    """Test that EmailVerification time_sent is populated."""
    print("Testing EmailVerification time_sent population...")
    
    # Create instances like in the tests
    user = get_user_model()()
    email = models.EmailAddress(address="test@example.com", user=user)
    verification = models.EmailVerification(email=email)
    
    print(f"Before send_email: time_sent = {verification.time_sent}")
    
    # Mock send_email to avoid actual email delivery
    with mock.patch('email_auth.models.email_utils.send_email') as mock_send_email:
        verification.send_email()
    
    print(f"After send_email: time_sent = {verification.time_sent}")
    
    if verification.time_sent is None:
        print("❌ BUG: time_sent is still None!")
        return False
    else:
        print("✅ time_sent was populated")
        return True


def test_password_reset_time_sent():
    """Test that PasswordReset time_sent is populated (should work)."""
    print("\nTesting PasswordReset time_sent population...")
    
    # Create instances like in the tests
    user = get_user_model()()
    email = models.EmailAddress(address="test@example.com", user=user)
    password_reset = models.PasswordReset(email=email)
    
    print(f"Before send_email: time_sent = {password_reset.time_sent}")
    
    # Mock send_email to avoid actual email delivery
    with mock.patch('email_auth.models.email_utils.send_email') as mock_send_email:
        password_reset.send_email()
    
    print(f"After send_email: time_sent = {password_reset.time_sent}")
    
    if password_reset.time_sent is None:
        print("❌ UNEXPECTED: PasswordReset time_sent is None!")
        return False
    else:
        print("✅ PasswordReset time_sent was populated")
        return True


if __name__ == '__main__':
    print("=" * 50)
    print("TESTING TIME_SENT POPULATION")
    print("=" * 50)
    
    # Test both models
    email_verification_ok = test_email_verification_time_sent()
    password_reset_ok = test_password_reset_time_sent()
    
    print("\n" + "=" * 50)
    print("RESULTS:")
    print(f"EmailVerification time_sent: {'OK' if email_verification_ok else 'BROKEN'}")
    print(f"PasswordReset time_sent: {'OK' if password_reset_ok else 'BROKEN'}")
    print("=" * 50)
    
    if not email_verification_ok and password_reset_ok:
        print("🐛 BUG CONFIRMED: EmailVerification is broken but PasswordReset works")
    elif email_verification_ok and password_reset_ok:
        print("✅ Both work correctly")

"""
Test to reproduce the time_sent issue in EmailVerification.
"""

from unittest import mock
from django.contrib.auth import get_user_model
from django.utils import timezone
from email_auth import models


def test_email_verification_time_sent():
    """Test that EmailVerification time_sent is populated."""
    print("Testing EmailVerification time_sent population...")
    
    # Create instances like in the tests
    user = get_user_model()()
    email = models.EmailAddress(address="test@example.com", user=user)
    verification = models.EmailVerification(email=email)
    
    print(f"Before send_email: time_sent = {verification.time_sent}")
    
    # Mock send_email to avoid actual email delivery
    with mock.patch('email_auth.models.email_utils.send_email') as mock_send_email:
        verification.send_email()
    
    print(f"After send_email: time_sent = {verification.time_sent}")
    
    if verification.time_sent is None:
        print("❌ BUG: time_sent is still None!")
        return False
    else:
        print("✅ time_sent was populated")
        return True


def test_password_reset_time_sent():
    """Test that PasswordReset time_sent is populated (should work)."""
    print("\nTesting PasswordReset time_sent population...")
    
    # Create instances like in the tests
    user = get_user_model()()
    email = models.EmailAddress(address="test@example.com", user=user)
    password_reset = models.PasswordReset(email=email)
    
    print(f"Before send_email: time_sent = {password_reset.time_sent}")
    
    # Mock send_email to avoid actual email delivery
    with mock.patch('email_auth.models.email_utils.send_email') as mock_send_email:
        password_reset.send_email()
    
    print(f"After send_email: time_sent = {password_reset.time_sent}")
    
    if password_reset.time_sent is None:
        print("❌ UNEXPECTED: PasswordReset time_sent is None!")
        return False
    else:
        print("✅ PasswordReset time_sent was populated")
        return True


def test_main():
    """Main test function."""
    print("=" * 50)
    print("TESTING TIME_SENT POPULATION")
    print("=" * 50)
    
    # Test both models
    email_verification_ok = test_email_verification_time_sent()
    password_reset_ok = test_password_reset_time_sent()
    
    print("\n" + "=" * 50)
    print("RESULTS:")
    print(f"EmailVerification time_sent: {'OK' if email_verification_ok else 'BROKEN'}")
    print(f"PasswordReset time_sent: {'OK' if password_reset_ok else 'BROKEN'}")
    print("=" * 50)
    
    if not email_verification_ok and password_reset_ok:
        print("🐛 BUG CONFIRMED: EmailVerification is broken but PasswordReset works")
        return False
    elif email_verification_ok and password_reset_ok:
        print("✅ Both work correctly")
        return True
    else:
        print("❓ Unexpected state")
        return False

## Phase 5. FIX ANALYSIS<tool_call>think<arg_key>thought</arg_key><arg_value>Now I have a clear understanding of the problem and solution:

**Problem Statement:**
The EmailVerification.send_email() method sends an email but never populates the time_sent field or saves the instance, unlike the PasswordReset.send_email() method which does both correctly.

**Problem Location:**
File: /workspace/cdriehuys__django-simple-email-auth__0.2/email_auth/models.py
Method: EmailVerification.send_email() (lines 254-272)

**How the Test Reproduces the Issue:**
My test creates an EmailVerification instance, calls send_email(), and checks if time_sent is populated. It shows that time_sent remains None, confirming the bug.

**Best Practices to Take Into Account:**
1. Follow the exact same pattern as PasswordReset.send_email()
2. Use timezone.now() for consistent timestamps
3. Call self.save() to persist changes to the database
4. Make minimal changes - only add the missing lines
5. Don't modify test files (as instructed)

**How to Fix the Problem:**
Add these two lines at the end of EmailVerification.send_email(), just like in PasswordReset.send_email():
```python
self.time_sent = timezone.now()
self.save()
```

This will:
1. Set the time_sent field to the current timestamp
2. Save the instance to persist the change
3. Match the behavior of PasswordReset.send_email()</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>Your thought has been logged.</tool_response><|assistant|></think>### 5.1 Problem Statement:

from unittest import mock
from django.contrib.auth import get_user_model
from django.utils import timezone

from email_auth import models


@mock.patch("email_auth.models.EmailVerification.save", autospec=True)
@mock.patch("email_auth.models.email_utils.send_email", autospec=True)
@mock.patch("email_auth.models.timezone.now", autospec=True)

    """Test that EmailVerification time_sent is populated."""
    print("Testing EmailVerification time_sent population...")
    
    # Create instances like in the tests
    user = get_user_model()()
    email = models.EmailAddress(address="test@example.com", user=user)
    verification = models.EmailVerification(email=email)
    
    print(f"Before send_email: time_sent = {verification.time_sent}")
    
    # Call send_email
    verification.send_email()
    
    print(f"After send_email: time_sent = {verification.time_sent}")
    
    # Verify that time_sent was set correctly
    assert verification.time_sent == mock_now.return_value
    assert mock_save.call_count == 1
    
    print("✅ time_sent was populated correctly")
    return True

"""
Test to reproduce the time_sent issue in EmailVerification.
"""

from unittest import mock
from django.contrib.auth import get_user_model
from django.utils import timezone
from email_auth import models


def test_email_verification_time_sent():
    """Test that EmailVerification time_sent is populated."""
    print("Testing EmailVerification time_sent population...")
    
    # Create instances like in the tests
    user = get_user_model()()
    email = models.EmailAddress(address="test@example.com", user=user)
    verification = models.EmailVerification(email=email)
    
    print(f"Before send_email: time_sent = {verification.time_sent}")
    
    # Mock send_email to avoid actual email delivery
    with mock.patch('email_auth.models.email_utils.send_email') as mock_send_email:
        verification.send_email()
    
    print(f"After send_email: time_sent = {verification.time_sent}")
    
    if verification.time_sent is None:
        print("❌ BUG: time_sent is still None!")
        return False
    else:
        print("✅ time_sent was populated")
        return True


def test_password_reset_time_sent():
    """Test that PasswordReset time_sent is populated (should work)."""
    print("\nTesting PasswordReset time_sent population...")
    
    # Create instances like in the tests
    user = get_user_model()()
    email = models.EmailAddress(address="test@example.com", user=user)
    password_reset = models.PasswordReset(email=email)
    
    print(f"Before send_email: time_sent = {password_reset.time_sent}")
    
    # Mock send_email to avoid actual email delivery
    with mock.patch('email_auth.models.email_utils.send_email') as mock_send_email:
        password_reset.send_email()
    
    print(f"After send_email: time_sent = {password_reset.time_sent}")
    
    if password_reset.time_sent is None:
        print("❌ UNEXPECTED: PasswordReset time_sent is None!")
        return False
    else:
        print("✅ PasswordReset time_sent was populated")
        return True


def test_main():
    """Main test function."""
    print("=" * 50)
    print("TESTING TIME_SENT POPULATION")
    print("=" * 50)
    
    # Test both models
    email_verification_ok = test_email_verification_time_sent()
    password_reset_ok = test_password_reset_time_sent()
    
    print("\n" + "=" * 50)
    print("RESULTS:")
    print(f"EmailVerification time_sent: {'OK' if email_verification_ok else 'BROKEN'}")
    print(f"PasswordReset time_sent: {'OK' if password_reset_ok else 'BROKEN'}")
    print("=" * 50)
    
    if not email_verification_ok and password_reset_ok:
        print("🐛 BUG CONFIRMED: EmailVerification is broken but PasswordReset works")
        return False
    elif email_verification_ok and password_reset_ok:
        print("✅ Both work correctly")

from django.conf import settings
from django.contrib.auth import get_user_model
from django.utils import timezone

from email_auth import models
from email_auth.test import test_utils


def test_init_all_fields():
    """
    Test creating an email verification with all allowed fields.
    """
    verification = models.EmailVerification(
        email=models.EmailAddress(), time_sent=timezone.now()
    )

    assert verification.pk


def test_repr():
    """
    The string representation of an instance should contain all the
    information necessary to recreate it.
    """
    email = models.EmailAddress(
        address="test@example.com", user=get_user_model()()
    )
    verification = models.EmailVerification(
        email=email, time_sent=timezone.now()
    )
    expected = test_utils.create_expected_repr(
        verification,
        ["email", "time_created", "time_sent", "time_updated", "token"],
    )

    assert repr(verification) == expected


@mock.patch("email_auth.models.email_utils.send_email", autospec=True)
def test_send_email(mock_send_email):
    """
    Sending an email should use django-email-utils to send the
    verification token to the user.
    """
    user = get_user_model()()
    email = models.EmailAddress(address="test@example.com", user=user)
    verification = models.EmailVerification(email=email)

    verification.send_email()

    assert mock_send_email.call_args[1] == {
        "context": {
            "email": email,
            "user": user,
            "verification": verification,
        },
        "from_email": settings.DEFAULT_FROM_EMAIL,
        "recipient_list": [email.address],
        "subject": "Please Verify Your Email Address",
        "template_name": "email_auth/emails/verify-email",
    }


def test_str():
    """
    Converting a verification instance to a string should return a
    message stating which email address the verification is for.
    """
    email = models.EmailAddress(address="test@example.com")
    verification = models.EmailVerification(email=email)
    expected = f"Verification for {email}"

    assert str(verification) == expected


def test_verify():
    """
    Verifying an instance should mark the associated email as verified
    and delete the verification instance.
    """
    email = models.EmailAddress()
    verification = models.EmailVerification(email=email)

    with mock.patch.object(
        email, "verify", autospec=True
    ) as mock_verify, mock.patch.object(
        verification, "delete", autospec=True
    ) as mock_delete:
        verification.verify()

    assert mock_verify.call_count == 1
    assert mock_delete.call_count == 1

Final verification that the EmailVerification time_sent fix works correctly.

from unittest import mock
from django.contrib.auth import get_user_model
from django.utils import timezone
from email_auth import models


def test_comprehensive_time_sent_fix():
    """Comprehensive test of the time_sent fix."""
    print("🧪 COMPREHENSIVE TIME_SENT FIX VERIFICATION")
    print("=" * 50)
    
    # Test 1: Basic functionality with proper mocking
    print("\n1. Testing basic time_sent population with proper mocking...")
    
    @mock.patch("email_auth.models.EmailVerification.save", autospec=True)
    @mock.patch("email_auth.models.email_utils.send_email", autospec=True)
    @mock.patch("email_auth.models.timezone.now", autospec=True)
    def test_basic_functionality(mock_now, mock_send_email, mock_save):
        # Setup
        user = get_user_model()()
        email = models.EmailAddress(address="test@example.com", user=user)
        verification = models.EmailVerification(email=email)
        
        # Mock current time
        mock_now.return_value = timezone.now()
        
        # Execute
        verification.send_email()
        
        # Verify
        assert verification.time_sent == mock_now.return_value, "time_sent should be set to current time"
        assert mock_save.call_count == 1, "save() should be called exactly once"
        assert mock_send_email.called, "send_email should be called"
        
        print("   ✅ Basic functionality works correctly")
        return True
    
    # Test 2: Compare with PasswordReset (should work the same way)
    print("\n2. Comparing with PasswordReset pattern...")
    
    @mock.patch("email_auth.models.PasswordReset.save", autospec=True)
    @mock.patch("email_auth.models.email_utils.send_email", autospec=True)
    @mock.patch("email_auth.models.timezone.now", autospec=True)
    def test_password_reset_pattern(mock_now, mock_send_email, mock_save):
        # Setup
        user = get_user_model()()
        email = models.EmailAddress(address="test@example.com", user=user)
        password_reset = models.PasswordReset(email=email)
        
        # Mock current time
        mock_now.return_value = timezone.now()
        
        # Execute
        password_reset.send_email()
        
        # Verify
        assert password_reset.time_sent == mock_now.return_value, "PasswordReset time_sent should work"
        assert mock_save.call_count == 1, "PasswordReset save() should be called"
        
        print("   ✅ PasswordReset pattern works correctly")
        return True
    
    # Test 3: Verify the actual implementation follows the pattern
    print("\n3. Verifying implementation follows the correct pattern...")
    
    # Check that EmailVerification.send_email sets time_sent and calls save
    import inspect
    
    # Get the source code of the method
    source = inspect.getsource(models.EmailVerification.send_email)
    
    # Check for the required lines
    has_time_sent_assignment = "self.time_sent = timezone.now()" in source
    has_save_call = "self.save()" in source
    
    if has_time_sent_assignment and has_save_call:
        print("   ✅ Implementation correctly includes time_sent assignment and save() call")
    else:
        print("   ❌ Implementation missing required elements")
        print(f"      time_sent assignment: {has_time_sent_assignment}")
        print(f"      save() call: {has_save_call}")
        return False
    
    # Run the actual tests
    try:
        test_basic_functionality()
        test_password_reset_pattern()
        print("\n🎉 ALL TESTS PASSED!")
        print("✅ EmailVerification time_sent field is now properly populated and persisted")
        print("✅ Implementation follows the same pattern as PasswordReset")
        return True
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"\n❌ TEST FAILED: {e}")
        return False

    print("✅ EmailVerification time_sent is properly populated and persisted")


@mock.patch("email_auth.models.PasswordReset.save", autospec=True)
@mock.patch("email_auth.models.email_utils.send_email", autospec=True)
@mock.patch("email_auth.models.timezone.now", autospec=True)
def test_password_reset_pattern_consistency(mock_now, mock_send_email, mock_save):
    """Test that PasswordReset works the same way (for comparison)."""
    print("Testing PasswordReset pattern consistency...")
    
    # Setup
    user = get_user_model()()
    email = models.EmailAddress(address="test@example.com", user=user)
    password_reset = models.PasswordReset(email=email)
    
    # Mock current time
    mock_now.return_value = timezone.now()
    
    # Execute
    password_reset.send_email()
    
    # Verify
    assert password_reset.time_sent == mock_now.return_value, "PasswordReset time_sent should work"
    assert mock_save.call_count == 1, "PasswordReset save() should be called"
    
    print("✅ PasswordReset pattern works consistently")
